consisting of one (1) page, represents the entire and integrated agreement between 
the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and agreements, 
whether written or oral. 
 
v. 
Force Majeure.  Neither Party shall be liable to perform under the MOU if such 
failure arises out of causes beyond control and without the fault or negligence of 
said Party.  Such causes may include, but are not limited to, acts of God or the 
public enemy, fires, floods, epidemics, quarantine restrictions, freight embargoes, 
and unusually severe weather. 
 
vi. 
Notices.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU shall be in 
writing and shall be served upon the other Party by regular mail, by personal

vii.

entering into this MOU and the City fully retains all immunities and defenses

viii. 
Third Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any other 
individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be

Memorandum of Understanding  
between State Construction Department and City of Cheyenne 
Page 1 of 3 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING BETWEEN  
STATE CONSTRUCTION DEPARTMENT 
AND  
CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
1. 
Parties. The parties to this Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) are the State 
Construction Department (SCD), whose address is: 700 W. 21st Street, Cheyenne, WY 
82002, and the City of Cheyenne, whose address is:2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY 
82001. 
 
2. 
Purpose. The purpose of this MOU is to designate the City of Cheyenne, Municipal 
Building as the primary emergency evacuation assembly area for the SCD. 
 
3. 
Term of MOU. This MOU shall commence upon the day and date last signed and executed 
by the duly authorized representatives of the parties to this MOU and shall remain in full 
force and effect until terminated.  This MOU may be terminated, without cause, by either 
party upon thirty (30) days written notice, which notice shall be delivered by hand or by 
certified mail. 
 
4. 
Payment. No payment shall be made to either party by the other party as a result of this 
MOU. 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of SCD.   
 
A. 
During an emergency at the SCD Building that requires evacuation, a member of 
the SCD leadership team will notify the City of Cheyenne as soon as practicable 
that an evacuation is in progress at the SCD Building, and that employees will be 
proceeding to the Municipal Building for accountability. 
a. Primary City of Cheyenne phone number: 307-637-6263 
b. Secondary City of Cheyenne phone number: 307-638-4394 
 
B. 
SCD employees will meet at the Civic Commons Park during nice weather, and 
will only shelter inside the Municipal Building during inclement weather or 
hazardous conditions.  Employees will be permitted to use the restrooms as 
necessary. 
 
C. 
Upon notification of the City of Cheyenne or designee that the Municipal Building 
is not a suitable assembly area for all SCD employees, SCD employees will be 
instructed to relocate to their alternative assembly area (Civic Commons Park).  
City of Cheyenne employees will have precedence in using the Municipal Building 
during emergencies.  
 
D. 
All SCD employees will comply with the code of conduct of the Municipal 
Building and with instructions from City of Cheyenne staff, including instructions 
to relocate, so as to minimize any disruption to the Municipal Building. 
 
2:54 pm, Oct 13 2023

 
Memorandum of Understanding  
between State Construction Department and City of Cheyenne 
Page 2 of 3 
6. 
Responsibilities of the City of Cheyenne.  
 
A. 
The City of Cheyenne or designee will notify a member of the SCD leadership team 
if a condition exists that causes the Municipal Building to no longer be a suitable 
primary evacuation assembly area for the SCD employees. 
a. Primary SCD phone number: 307-287-5748 
b. Secondary SCD phone number: 307-631-2582 
c. Tertiary SCD phone number: 307-274-0941 
 
7. 
General Provisions. 
 
A. 
Amendments. Either party may request changes in this MOU.  Any changes, 
modifications, revisions, or amendments to this MOU which are mutually agreed 
upon by the parties to this MOU shall be incorporated by written instrument, 
executed and signed by all parties to this MOU. 
 
B. 
Applicable Law. The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this MOU 
shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of this MOU and over 
the parties, and the venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, 
Wyoming. 
 
C. 
Entirety of Agreement. This MOU, consisting of three (2) pages, represents the 
entire and integrated agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior 
negotiations, representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
D. 
Prior Approval. This MOU shall not be binding upon either party unless this MOU 
has been reduced to writing before performance begins as described under the terms 
of this MOU, and unless this MOU is approved as to form by the Attorney General 
or her representative. 
 
E. 
Severability. Should any portion of this MOU be judicially determined to be illegal 
or unenforceable, the remainder of the MOU shall continue in full force and effect, 
and the parties may renegotiate the terms affected by the severance. 
 
F. 
Sovereign Immunity. The State of Wyoming, SCD and City of Cheyenne do not 
waive sovereign or governmental immunity by entering into this MOU, and each 
fully retains all immunities and defenses provided by law with respect to any action 
based on or occurring as a result of this MOU. 
 
G. 
Third Party Beneficiary Rights. The parties do not intend to create in any other 
individual or entity the status of third-party beneficiary, and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status.  The rights, duties, and obligations contained 
in this MOU shall operate only between the parties to this MOU and shall inure 
solely to the benefit of the parties to this MOU.  The provisions of this MOU are 

 
Memorandum of Understanding  
between State Construction Department and City of Cheyenne 
Page 3 of 3 
intended only to assist the parties in determining and performing their obligations 
under this MOU. 
 
8. 
Signatures.  The  parties to this MOU, through their duly authorized representatives, have 
executed this MOU on the dates set out below, and certify that they have read, understood, 
and agreed to the terms and conditions of this MOU as set forth herein. 
 
The Effective Date of this MOU is the date of the signature last affixed to this page.

1. 
Parties. The parties to this Recipient Agreement (Agreement) are the Wyoming
Department of Transportation, Local Government Coordination Office (WYDOT), whose
address is 5300 Bishop Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009, and the City of Cheyenne
Transit Program (Recipient), whose address is 322 West Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming
82001.
2. 
Purpose. This is an award of state financial assistance from WYDOT to the Recipient. The
purpose of this Agreement is to set forth the respective relationships and responsibilities of
the Recipient and WYDOT in the administration of the Wyoming Rural Public Transit
Program. The Recipient shall use state funds for the Rural Public Transit Program covering
the operations, administration, and/or maintenance costs associated with providing public
transportation in the Recipient's service area (Project) in Laramie County. All work
covered by this Agreement shall comply with the standards of the Americans with
Disabilities Act of 1990 (ADA).
Pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §§ 24-15-101 and 24-15-102, WYDOT is authorized to distribute
these program funds in accordance with the WYDOT State Management Plan and the
Master Agreement with the Federal Transit Administration (FTA) guidelines. This
Agreement is also subject to all the Certifications and Assurances required by the FTA.
WYDOT and the Recipient desire to secure and utilize grant funds for the public
transportation needs in small urban and rural areas in the State of Wyoming.
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program

3. 
Term of Agreement. This Agreement is effective when all parties have executed it
(Effective Date). The term of this Agreement is from October 1, 2023, through December
31, 2024. The Subrecipient's timeffame to complete the work under this agreement
(Budget Period) is from October 1, 2023, through September 30, 2024. The Period of
Performance is from October 1, 2023 through December 31, 2024.
4. 
Payment.
A. 
WYDOT agrees to reimburse the Recipient for the Project described in Section 2
above. The total reimbursement shall not exceed three hundred thousand dollars
($300,000.00). Total Project costs exceeding Project estimate of three hundred
thousand dollars ($300,000.00) (including local match) shall be borne by the
Recipient.
The Recipient's eligible costs incurred shall be reimbursed upon receipt of an
approved WYDOT reimbursement request form, which is provided in WYDOT's
project management system. WYDOT requires support documentation for any and
all submittals. WYDOT will reimburse those eligible costs based on the program
guidelines for the item categories and their appropriate reimbursement ratio.
Requests for reimbursement shall be submitted monthly. Reimbursement requests
submitted three months or more affer the expenses were incurred may not be
processed for payment. The final invoice for reimbursement will be accepted by
WYDOT no later than forty-five (45) days from September 30, 2024. The basis for
reimbursement shall be as follows:
(i) 
Net Operating Deficit (Total Operating Expense - 
Farebox = Net Operating
Deficit) is reimbursed with State funds at one hundred percent (100.00%),
with no Local Match.
The Recipient shall initiate and prosecute to completion all actions necessary to
enable the Recipient to provide its share of the Project costs at or prior to the time
that such funds are needed to meet Project costs. The Recipient shall provide
WYDOT monthly reporting of ridership and expenses, and semi-annual inventory
updates. The Recipient shall submit invoices in sufficient detail to ensure that
reimbursements may be made in conformance with this Agreement.
Reimbursement shall be made within forty-five (45) days after submission of
invoice pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602.
B. 
Should the Recipient fail to perform in a manner consistent with the terms and
conditions set forth in this Agreement, reimbursement under this Agreement may
be withheld until such time as the Recipient performs its duties and responsibilities
to the satisfaction of WYDOT.

between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program

C. 
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the Recipient shall pay all costs
and expenses, including travel, incurred by Recipient or on its behalf in connection
with Recipient's performance and compliance with all of Recipient's obligations
under this Agreement.
D. 
The Federal Funding Accountability and Transparency Act (FFATA) of 2006
requires that you provide your Unique Entity Identifier (UEI) number to WYDOT.
This requirement means you need to be registered with Sam.Gov to be assigned an
UEI number. Instructions for this process can be found at www.sam.gov.

and
http://edocket.access.gpo.gOv/2010/pdf/2010-22706.pdf.
5. 
Responsibilities of the Parties.
A. 
Project Scope. The Recipient shall undertake and complete the Project in
accordance with this Agreement.
(i) 
The Subrecipient shall utilize this grant for the operations, administration,
and/or maintenance costs associated with providing public transportation in
their service area.
(ii) 
The Recipient shall abide by the terms as described in Attachment A, Award
Letter, and Attachment B, FY 2024 Wyoming Rural Public Transit Annual
Program Update, which are attached to and incorporated into this
Agreement by this reference.
(iii) 
WYDOT agrees to reimburse the Recipient in accordance with Section 4
above.
B. 
Purchase of Project Equipment. The purchase of all Project equipment financed
in whole or in part pursuant to this Agreement shall be undertaken by WYDOT on
behalf of the Recipient or by the Recipient with prior written WYDOT approval in
accordance with the procedures established by WYDOT. Purchases of more than
four thousand, nine hundred ninety-nine dollars ($4,999.00) using Federal or State
funds are subject to WYDOT approval in accordance with established procedures.
(i) 
Title to Project Equipment. Title to Project Equipment shall be in the
name of the Recipient subject to the restrictions on use and disposition of
the Project Equipment set forth herein. The following notification shall be
typed on the face of the title document of each vehicle by the County Clerk:
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program
Page 3 of 17
osMiwr

"WYDOT Grant - 
Restricted." It is the responsibility of the Recipient to
ensure the County Clerk is notified of this requirement.
(u) 
Use of Project Equipment. The Recipient agrees that the Project
equipment, and/or facilities, shall be used for the purpose for which it was
acquired, as long as it is needed in the Project. If the Project equipment is
no longer needed, or cannot be used for the intended purpose, prior to selling
or otherwise disposing of the Project equipment, the Recipient shall
immediately notify WYDOT. Before selling or otherwise disposing of the
Project equipment, the Recipient must receive a written notification from
WYDOT allowing the sale or disposal. The Recipient shall not execute any
lease, pledge, mortgage, lien, or other contract touching or affecting the
State interest in any Project equipment, nor shall it obligate itself in any
other manner, with any third party with respect to the Project equipment,
unless such lease, pledge, mortgage, lien, contract, or other obligation is
either in conformance with the standards of FTA guidelines or is otherwise
expressly authorized in writing by WYDOT, nor shall the Recipient or any
other organization that obtains facilities or equipment through this
Agreement, by any act or omission of any kind, impair its continuing control
over the use of Project equipment. The Recipient shall maintain satisfactory
records with regard to the use of the property and submit to WYDOT upon
request such information as is required in order to assure compliance with
this section. The Recipient shall immediately notify WYDOT in all cases
where Project equipment is used in a manner substantially different from
that described in the Project description. The Recipient shall maintain an
amount and form satisfactory to WYDOT such insurance or self-insurance
as will be adequate to protect Project equipment throughout the period of
required use. During the Period of Performance, the Recipient shall
maintain the Project equipment at a high level of cleanliness, safety and
mechanical soundness. The Recipient shall maintain equipment in
accordance with its WYDOT approved Maintenance Plan adopted by its
Board or Governing Body and on file with WYDOT. WYDOT and the FTA
shall have the right to monitor maintenance records in order to confirm that
proper upkeep has been conducted pursuant to this section.
C. 
Prohibited Interest. No member, officer, or employee of the Recipient during his
or her tenure or one (1) year thereafter shall have any interest, direct or indirect, in
this Agreement or the proceeds thereof.
D. 
Certifications and Assurances. The Recipient acknowledges that they have read
and agree to comply with all requirements in the current FTA Master Agreement
and the current FTA Certifications and Assurances. Recipient shall certify that they
have read and agree to these requirements annually.
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-14I
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program

Dmnsi

E. 
Restrictions, Prohibitions, Controls, and Labor Provisions.
(!) 
Equal Employment Opportunity. In connection with carrying out of the
Project, the Recipient shall not discriminate against any employee or
applicant for employment because of race, color, age, creed, sex, national
origin, or disability. The Recipient shall take affirmative action to ensure
that applicants are employed, and that employees are treated during
employment, without regard to their race, color, creed, sex, age, national
origin, or disability. Such action shall include, but not be limited to, the
following: employment, upgrading, demotion or transfer, recruitment or
recruitment advertising, layoff or termination; rates of pay or other forms of
compensation; and selection for training, including apprenticeship.
(ii) 
Disadvantaged Business Enterprise (DBE) Requirements.
(a) 
Policy. It is the policy of WYDOT that DBEs, defined as Minority
Business Enterprises and Woman Business Enterprises, shall have
the maximum opportunity to participate in the performance of
contracts financed in whole or in part with funds under this
Agreement. Consequently, the DBE requirements of 49 CFR Part
26 applies to this Agreement.
(b) 
DBE Obligation. The Recipient or its contractor agrees to ensure
that DBEs as defined in 49 CFR Part 26 have the maximum
opportunity to participate in the performance of contracts and
subcontracts financed in whole or in part with funds provided under
this Agreement. In this regard, all Recipients shall take all necessary
and reasonable steps in accordance with 49 CFR Part 26 to ensure
that DBEs have the maximum opportunity to compete for and
perform contracts. The Recipient and their contractors shall not
discriminate on the basis of race, color, national origin, or sex in the
award and performance of WYDOT assisted contracts.
(ill) 
Title VI Civil Rights Act of 1964. The Recipient shall comply and shall
assure the compliance by contractors and subcontractors under this Project
with all the requirements imposed by Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of
1964 (49 U.S.C. 2000d), the Regulations of the Department of
Transportation (DOT) issued there under, 49 CFR Part 21, and the
Assurance by the Recipient pursuant thereto.
(iv) 
Compliance with Elderlv and Disabled Regulations. The Recipient shall
comply with applicable regulations regarding transportation for elderly and
disabled persons, set forth in 49 CFR Part 27 and the Americans with
Disabilities Act of 1990.
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FV 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program

(v) 
Domestic Preferences for Procurement. Requires a preference for the
purchase, acquisition, or use of goods, products or materials produced in the
United States (including but not limited to iron, aluminum, steel, cement,
and other manufactured products) in accordance to 2 CFR 200.322.
(vi) 
Prohibition of Certain Telecommunications and Video Surveillance
Services or Equipment. Federal law prohibits procuring, obtaining,
extending, renewing or entering into contracts for equipment or services
from manufacturers listed in 2 CFR 200.216.
(vii) 
Never Contract with the Enemv: Awarding agencies and Recipients are
subject to the regulations implementing Never Contract with the Enemy in
2CFRpart 183. The regulations in2CFRpart 183 affect covered contracts,
grants, and cooperative agreements that are expected to exceed fifty
thousand dollars ($50,000.00) within the period of performance, are
performed outside the United States and its territories, and are in support of
a eontingency operation in which members ofthe Armed Forces are actively
engaged in hostilities in aecordance to 2 CFR 200.215.
(viii) The Recipient agrees that 49 U.S.C. § 5333 - Labor Standards apply to this
Agreement. The Certifications and Warranties are available from the FTA
and WYDOT upon request and are incorporated by reference into this
Agreement. Below is a brief summary of the requirements as well as
program applicability.
(a) 
Prevailing Wage Requirements. Construction work financed in
whole or part by the FTA shall require that the provisions of Davis-
Bacon Act apply.
(b) 
Employee Protective Arrangements. As a condition of 49 U.S.C. §§
5307-5312, 5337 and/or 5339, employers are required to provide
workers with basic protections as required by the Department of
Labor. This includes the Special Warranty; details can be found on
the 
Department 
of 
Labor's 
website 
at
httt)://www.dol.gov/olms/regs/compliance/compltransit.htm or may
be obtained by contacting the Department of Labor at (202) 693-
0126.
An employer who receives transit funds must protect all covered
transit employees affected by the use of the money. The Department
of Labor must approve the arrangements made to protect these
employees. For covered employees the arrangements include:
(1) 
preserving their rights and benefits;
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program
Page 6 of 17

(2) 
continuing their collective bargaining rights;
(3) 
protecting them against a worsening of their employment
conditions;
(4) 
assuring jobs for employees of acquired mass transit
systems;
(5) 
providing priority of reemployment if the employee is laid
off or his job is eliminated; and
(6) 
providing paid training.
F. 
Motor Vehicle Safety.
(i) 
Motor Vehicle Safety Standards. The motor vehicles will comply with the
Motor Vehicle Safety Standards and Pollution requirements as established
by the United States Department of Transportation.
(ii) 
Motor Vehicle Requirements. When new motor vehicles are purchased,
there shall be obtained from each vendor a certification in writing that: "The
horsepower of the vehicle is adequate for the speed, range, and terrain in
which it will be required to operate and also to meet the demands of all
auxiliary power equipment."
G. 
Nondiscrimmation on the Basis of Disability - 504 Regulations.
(i) 
The Recipient shall ensure to WYDOT that persons with disabilities and
groups representing them have had adequate notice of and opportunity to
comment on the present and proposed plans for providing transportation to
disabled persons unable to use general public service.
(ii) 
The Recipient must certify to WYDOT that special efforts are being made
to provide transportation to eligible disabled persons. This service must be
reasonable in cost, meet a significant fraction for such transportation needs,
and be provided within a reasonable time.
(iii) 
The Recipient shall submit a status report to WYDOT 
(updated every three
(3) years). The report must include:
(a) 
A description of the service to the disabled, as compared to the
general public service;
(b) 
Copies or a summary of the comments received in response to the
opportunity to comment;
(c) 
A statement of any plans to significantly modify the services; and
(d) 
A statement of the resources devoted to the disabled service.
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program
Page 7 of 17
MWIH

H. 
Safety Jurisdiction. FTA requires that all Recipients operate their service in a safe
manner and not allow any conditions to develop that create a serious hazard of death
or injury.
(i) 
Safety Training Classes are a mandated requirement of the Safety
Jurisdiction clause. Driver Safety and Passenger Assistance Training (TSI)
or equal approved training, ADA compliance training, and other technical
training programs are required on a regular basis (as specified by the State
Management Plan). The Wyoming Public Transit 
Association
(WYTRANS) 
shall maintain records and files of training information. The
Recipient shall maintain independent employee training records for its file
as well. The Recipient shall notify WYTRANS of any employee-needed
training to keep current with the requirements of this section. If safety
violations are found and a Recipient fails to correct them, or comply with
the training mandate, further financial assistance may be withheld.
I. 
Emergency Management. All Recipients are required by WYDOT to cooperate
and coordinate with Emergency Management agencies in their area during natural
or man-made disasters. All FTA vehicles shall be used to transport people and/or
equipment as directed by WYDOT. All Recipients shall use their communication
equipment to ensure timely evacuation of elderly and disabled people in their area.
J. 
Records and Reports.
(i) 
Establishment and Maintenance of Accounting Records. The Recipient
shall establish and maintain, in accordance with requirements established
by WYDOT, separate accounts for the Project, either independently or
within its existing accounting system, to be known as the Project Account.
(ii) 
Documentation of Project Costs. All charges to the Project Account shall
be supported by properly executed invoices, contracts or vouchers
evidencing in proper detail the nature and propriety of the charges, in
accordance with WYDOT rules and regulations. Copies and/or supporting
documentation of such invoices, contracts or vouchers may be included
with monthly billing statements to cover all charges submitted for payment.
The original documentation should be maintained by the Recipient in the
Project file and must be available to WYDOT upon request.
(iii) 
Checks, Orders and Vouchers. Any check or order drawn by the Recipient
with respect to any item which is or will be chargeable against the Project
Account will be drawn only in accordance with a properly signed voucher
then on file in the office of the Recipient stating in proper detail the purpose
for which such check or order is drawn. All checks, invoices, contracts,
vouchers, orders or other accounting documents pertaining in whole or in
Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program

part to the Project shall be clearly identified, readily accessible and, to the
extent feasible, kept separate and apart from all other such documents.
(iv) 
Reports. The Recipient shall advise WYDOT regarding the progress of the
Project at such times and in such manner as WYDOT may require,
including, but not limited to meetings and interim reports. The Recipient
shall submit, accompanying the monthly reimbursement report, ridership
data reports on approved WYDOT forms. WYDOT will not process
requests for reimbursement without completed ridership data for the period
of the reimbursement request. The Recipient shall submit to WYDOT, or
its authorized representatives, an inventory of all vehicles, facilities, and
equipment purchased by the Recipient as part of the Project; all
transportation services rendered by the Recipient by the use of such
vehicles, facilities and equipment; and all relevant Project data and records.
The Recipient shall also permit the above-named persons to examine the
Recipient's books, records and accounts pertaining to the Project. If the
Recipient has an audit performed for its own purposes, the Recipient agrees
to provide WYDOT with a copy of such audit report. The Recipient shall
keep records and audit reports on file for three (3) years after the close of
its fiscal year.
6. 
Special Provisions.
A. 
Assumption of Risk. The Recipient shall assume the risk of any loss of state or
federal funding, either administrative or program dollars, due to the Recipient's
failure to comply with state or federal requirements. WYDOT shall notify the
Recipient of any state or federal determination of noncompliance.
B. 
Conflict of Interest. Per 2 CFR 200.112, the Recipient must disclose in writing
any potential conflict of interest to WYDOT including financial or other personal
interests.
C. 
Environmental Policy Acts. The Recipient agrees all activities under this
Agreement will comply with the Clean Air Act, the Clean Water Act, the National
Environmental Policy Act, and other related provisions of federal environmental
protection laws, rules, or regulations.
D. 
Human Trafficking. As required by 22 U.S.C. § 7104(g) and 2 CFR Part 175, this
Agreement may be terminated without penalty if a private entity that receives funds
under this Agreement:
(i) 
Engages in severe forms of trafficking in persons during the period of time
that the award is in effect;

Kickbacks. The Recipient certifies and warrants that no gratuities, kickbacks, or
contingency fees were paid in connection with this Agreement, nor were any fees,
commissions, gifts, or other considerations made contingent upon the award of this
Agreement. If the Recipient breaches or violates this warranty, WYDOT may, at
its discretion, terminate this Agreement without liability to WYDOT, or deduct
from the agreed upon price or consideration, or otherwise recover, the full amount
of any commission, percentage, brokerage, or contingency fee. The Recipient shall
comply with the Copeland Anti-Kickback Act (18 U.S.C. § 874) as supplemented
in the Department of Labor Regulations (29 CFR, Part 3). This Act provides that
the Recipient is prohibited from inducing, by any means, any person employed in
the construction, completion, or repair of public works to give up any part of the
compensation to which he or she is otherwise entitled.
F. 
Limitations on Lobbying Activities. By signing this Agreement, the Recipient
certifies and agrees that, in accordance with P.L. 101-121, payments made from a
federal grant shall not be utilized by the Recipient or its subcontractor in connection
with lobbying member(s) of Congress, or any federal agency in connection with the
award of a federal grant, contract, cooperative agreement, or loan. The Recipient
and subcontractors may also be required to submit an additional certification
statement and disclosure form acceptable to WYDOT before commencement of the
work.
G. 
Mandatory Disclosures. Per 2 CFR 200.113, the Recipient must disclose, in a
timely manner, in writing, to WYDOT all violations of Federal criminal law
involving fraud, bribery, or gratuity violations potentially affecting this award.
Failure to make required disclosures can result in remedies for noncompliance
including suspension or debarment.
H. 
Monitoring Activities. WYDOT shall have the right to monitor all activities
related to this Agreement that are performed by the Recipient or its subcontractors.
This shall include, but not be limited to, the right to make site inspections at any
time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts and consultants on site to examine
or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to examine the books, ledgers,
documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; and, to observe
personnel in every phase of performance of the related work.
I. 
Nondiscrimination. The Recipient shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964,
the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the

Federal Audit Requirements. The Recipient agrees that if it expends an aggregate

Non-Supplanting Certification. The Recipient hereby affirms that federal grant

Program Income. The Recipient shall not deposit grant funds in an interest bearing

funds distributed under this Agreement must be used to increase the scope of the

General Provisions.
A. 
Amendments. Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to this
Agreement which are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this Agreement shall
be incorporated by written instrument, executed by all parties to this Agreement.

Applicable Law, Rules of Construction, and Venue. The construction,
interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of
the State of Wyoming, without regard to conflicts of law principles. The terms
"hereof," "hereunder," "herein," and words of similar import, are intended to refer
to this Agreement as a whole and not to any particular provision or part. The Courts
of the State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this Agreement and the parties.
The venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming.
C. 
Assignment Prohibited and Agreement Shall Not be used as Collateral. Neither
party shall assign or otherwise transfer any of the rights or delegate any of the duties
set out in this Agreement without the prior written consent of the other party. The
Recipient shall not use this Agreement, or any portion thereof, for collateral for any
financial obligation without the prior written permission of WYDOT.
D. 
Audit and Access to Records. WYDOT and its representatives shall have access
to any books, documents, papers, electronic data, and records of the Recipient
which are pertinent to this Agreement. The Recipient shall immediately, upon

any audit authorized by WYDOT.

Availability of Funds. Each payment obligation of WYDOT is conditioned upon
the availability of government funds which are appropriated or allocated for the

WYDOT shall not be obligated or liable for any future payments due or for any

Award of Related Agreements. WYDOT may award supplemental or successor
contracts for work related to this Agreement or may award contracts to other

with other Recipients and WYDOT in all such cases.

Compliance with Laws. The Recipient shall keep informed of and comply with all
applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations, and all federal grant
requirements and executive orders in the performance of this Agreement.

Confidentiality of Information. Except when disclosure is required by the
Wyoming Public Records Act or court order, all documents, data compilations,
reports, computer programs, photographs, data, and other work provided to or
produced by the Recipient in the performance of this Agreement shall be kept
confidential by the Recipient unless written permission is granted by WYDOT for
its release. If and when Recipient receives a request for information subject to this
Agreement, Recipient shall notify WYDOT within ten (10) days of such request
and shall not release such information to a third party unless directed to do so by
WYDOT.

Extensions. Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted or deemed to create an

Force Majeure. Neither party shall be liable for failure to perform under this

without the fault or negligence of the nonperforming party. Such causes may
include, but are not limited to, acts of God or the public enemy, fires, floods,

weather. This provision shall become effective only if the party failing to perform
immediately notifies the other party of the extent and nature of the problem, limits
delay in performance to that required by the event, and takes all reasonable steps to
minimize delays.

Indemnification. Each party to this Agreement shall assume the risk of any liability
arising from its own conduct. Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify
the other.

Independent Contractor. The Recipient shall function as an independent
contractor for the purposes of this Agreement and shall not be considered an

the details of the performance of services under this Agreement. The Recipient shall
assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that may be incurred by the
Recipient in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement and shall be solely responsible
for the payment of all federal, state, and local taxes which may accrue because of
this Agreement. Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the
Recipient or its agents or employees to act as an agent or representative for or on
behalf of the State of Wyoming or WYDOT or to incur any obligation of any kind
on behalf of the State of Wyoming or WYDOT. The Recipient agrees that no health

  Equal Employment Opportunity: 
How many Transit-Related Employees are at your agency? 
31 
The table below includes examples of how to count transit-related employees: 
● A city planner involved in planning bus routes would be counted as part of the transit-
related workforce, but a city planner involved only in land use would not be counted. 
● A budget position who processes payments or does any financial processes for the 
transit program in addition to other responsibilities for the agency. 
● An employee focusing on grants management who handles grant applications for 
several town programs, including transit programs. 
● An HR officer managing staffing at the transit agency or managing transit staff as part 
of a city or county program. 
● An employee providing civil rights oversight at a transit agency. 
● A security guard or facilities staffer at a transit-specific building would be a transit-
related employee.

program.  Please be prepared to explain how you arrived at the number you have entered

Please explain your ADA Complaint process: 
To resolve complaints promptly and confidentially all complaints, regardless of how they are received i.e., 
telephone, mail, in person, or email, are directed to the Transit Manager for investigation.  CTP has a 
Suggestions/Compliments/Complaints Form. 
 
When Cheyenne Transit receives an ADA complaint, the Transit Manager will initiate a thorough and timely 
investigation.  A written report of the investigation may be prepared which shall summarize investigatory 
actions and a summary of results of the investigation.  From the investigation results, a determination will be 
made as to what action is warranted and provide follow-up. 
Fixed Route Service (If Applicable): 
Describe the Fixed Route Service you operate 
(description of service area and stops). 
We are not currently operating fixed-route service, but 
will be in the near future. 
How will you meet the ADA-Specified Complementary 
Paratransit requirements as outlined in 49 CFR 37 for 
service within ¾ mile of your route? Please make 
sure a copy of your Complementary Para-Transit 
Plan is uploaded in the Resources tab in BlackCat. 
During normal operations, we provide paratransit services 
with a service boundary that exceeds the ¾ mile radius.   
 
Demand Response Service: 
Describe the Demand Response Service you operate 
(description of service area). 
We provide on-demand service to all members of the 
public within our service area. 
How will you ensure the service is available to the 
general public? 
Members of the general public can call our office or 
download the Cheyenne Transit App from their Appstore 
to schedule a trip. 
What percentage of riders do you anticipate will be 
general public? 
60% 

 
 
 
 
Attachment B to the Wyoming Rural Public Transit Program FY 2024 Recipient Agreement No. RPT-24-141 
 
between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne Transit Program 
Page 4 of 6 
Do you deliver meals as part of your transportation 
program? Home Delivery Meals should not interfere 
with the operation of your transit service. 
No 
How do you prioritize rides? Ex: Will the general 
public be bumped from a ride if the bus is full of 
senior citizens? 
First come, first served. 
 
Deviated Fixed Route Service: 
Describe what makes your service a Deviated Route. 
N/A 
Training: 
Do all your drivers have the required training? 
Yes 
If you answered NO to the above question, please 
provide an explanation. 
Describe what makes your service a Deviated Route. 
N/A 
Are all your trainings current? 
Yes 
Describe your proposed training schedule for the 
upcoming fiscal year. 
CPR and First Aid training classes are scheduled every two 
months throughout the year.  TSI Driver training classes 
are scheduled at least three times a year.  All required 
training is completed annually. 
Program Future: 
Describe any community or economic changes taking 
place that you anticipate will affect your transit 
service in the next five years. 
The city is growing, and new areas are being annexed that 
will eventually require transit service. 
 
Is your agency considering increasing or decreasing 
service in the upcoming fiscal year? 
Recently adopted Transit Development Plan calls for 
starting up newly defined route service, continuing ADA 
Paratransit, and continuing on-demand service. 
Vehicle and Facility Insurance: 
What is the total anticipated cost of vehicle 
insurance? 
$28,121.00 
What is the anticipated cost of your transit facility 
insurance (if applicable)? 
$20,879.00

Transportation Voucher Program 
 
The Transportation Voucher Program is a system designed to assist people with disabilities in 
meeting their transportation needs.  Funding must support projects that are aimed at 
providing additional tools to overcome existing barriers facing persons with disabilities 
seeking integration into the workforce and full participation in society.

The Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) is committed to ensuring that no person is excluded from participation in, or 
denied the benefits of, or be subject to discrimination in the receipt of its services based on race, color, or national origin, or any other 
characteristics protected by law, including Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, as amended. Further, under the N/A Americans with 
Disabilities Act (ADA) of 1990, no entity shall discriminate against any individual with a physical or mental disability in connection with 
the provision of transportation services.

Mission and Goals of FFY 2024 Title III-B Supportive Services Program
Title 111-B Program enables older individuals, 60 and older, to remain physically, mentally and socially active
through services designed to meet their needs. This program helps older adult individuals, 60 and older, to remain
in their own homes by providing supportive services, to enhance their health and to be active contributors to their
families and communities.
Scope of the Program - 
The provision of any or all Title III-B services as described in Section 321 of the Older
Americans Act; with emphasis on identifying and serving low income, rural, minority and limited English
proficient older individuals. These services categories may include Health Services, Socialization, Support
Services including Information & Assistance, and Transportation and a wide variety of other supportive services
that enrich and stabilize the lives of seniors.
Listed below are the allowable services and activities within the four Title Ill-B categories of services: Health
Services, Socialization Services, Support Services, and Transportation Services. In the green box below each
category of service, you must select: "YES" this services that WILL be provided, or "NO" this services that
WILL NOT be provided. Then, mark "YES" or "NO" in the shaded box(s) of service(s) under the category
of services you selected to provide (that you marked "YES" in the green boxes).

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-10-03_TotalExtraction_83166044.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
Subject: A development agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne, Wyoming, a 
municipal corporation and Goat Systems, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company. 
Date: 
October 23, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a development agreement (DA) related to a development within the High 
Plains Business Park. This DA is related to a project, commonly known as “Project Cosmo,” within the 
business park. The High Plains Business Park final plat was recorded on January 24, 2023. 
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: October 23, 2023 
• 
Public Service Committee: November 7, 2023 
• 
Final Consideration: November 13, 2023 
 
Review of the DA and related documents has been occurring over the course of the last year and 
was preceded by a memorandum of understanding (MOU) which was approved by the Governing 
Body on February 27, 2023. The developer, Goat Systems, LLC, proposes an approximate 945-acre 
data center campus development. The developer will be required to pay park fees, public safety 
fees, and building permit revenue and will aid in construction of on-site and off-site infrastructure 
(sewer, water, and roadways) to support the development and will provide avenues for 
development of nearby lands. The City’s intent of the DA is to provide assurances of anticipated 
fees and costs to the developer - not to prematurely grant the developer any specific approvals or 
exceptions to City requirements.    
 
Bringing forward a DA prior to completion is not a typical action by the City. In working with this 
developer, they have indicated that a timely review by the Governing Body is necessary and 
advancing this DA in this manner is the least disruptive to their timeline. Project representatives 
and the City have nearly reached agreement on the content and form of the DA and intend to 
provide a final copy of the DA to the Public Services Committee by November 3, 2023.      
 
Staff will provide a recommendation at the Public Services Committee meeting.

The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
I. Description of Changes
The City of Cheyenne and Novo Benefits, LLC, entered into a professional services agreement 
dated February 14, 2018 (City Contract no. 6786). The original agreement’s completion date is 
December 31, 2023. The Parties both agree that an additional three (3) year term is necessary to 
continue the successful long-term planning and stability in the City’s health coverage plan. 
II. New Contract Items
The Parties agree that the fee for this service shall be no greater than: seventy-three thousand 
seven hundred and thirty-three dollars ($73,733) beginning on January 1, 2023; seventy-five 
thousand nine hundred and forty-five dollars ($75,945) beginning on January 1, 2024; and 
seventy-eight thousand two hundred and twenty-three dollars ($78,223) beginning of January 1, 
2026.  Novo benefits shall bill the City of Cheyenne monthly in 1/12th increments. 
III. Schedule
The original contract is amended to extend the terms of the agreement from an additional three (3) 
years period, beginning on January 1, 2024 through December 31, 2027. The Agreement shall 
otherwise remain in full force and effect unless agreed to in writing by the parties hereto through 
December 31, 2027.

August 30, 2023 
 
City of Cheyenne Transit Department 
2101 O’Neil Ave. 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
SUBJECT: City of Cheyenne Transit Facility Change Order #2 
 
Dear Renae, 
 
We have been working diligently on the Transit Facility Site Plan. Approximately a month 
ago we submitted the final site plan and the construction plans to the city for the site 
plan review (SPR) and engineering plan review (EPR). At that time, we had expended 
most of our original design budget plus the approved amendment for the site plan 
assuming that any comments after the EPR and SPR would be relatively minimal. That 
was not the case and we have now addressed the comments for the EPR and SPR and 
resubmitted while incurring additional hours to complete the project. Additionally, as 
the EPR was processed we have been made aware that drainage easements will be 
required.  
To date we have billed $47,482.75 (through June 30th) of our $56,225.00 not to exceed 
contract and have incurred approximately an additional $8,200 in design fees in the 
month of August.  After resubmitting, we are hopeful that any comments from the city 
will be minor and that we can continue forward to bidding and construction 
administration of the project. As it currently stands, if we bill for our additional design 
effort, we will approach the not to exceed contract value and we still do not have an 
approved site plan or engineering plans. Additionally, we are still under contract to 
provide limited support during bidding and construction administration.  
I am requesting an increase to our not to exceed fee to account for the additional 
work required to resubmit the EPR and SPR, prepare the required drainage easements, 
and to provide some budget to account for minor final comments and assist with 
preparing the project for bid. I’ve provided a summary of what we are requesting 
below.  
 
 

August 30, 2023

Interceptors (SUV).

recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Ken Garff Cheyenne.

the only Certified Resident Contractor.  As such, after application of the 5% Residency Preference,

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-5-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and Arena 
Products and Services, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Cheyenne Ice and Events Center Evaporative Condenser 
Replacement” project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda 
thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated September 20, 2023. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by April 30, 2024.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Six Thousand, One Hundred Fifty-Three Dollars and Fifteen Cents ($106,153.15).  
In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated 
work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due 
date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be 
submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA 
Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to 
payment. 
11:16 am, Oct 17 2023

Page 2 of 2 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.
